        "code": "## 0. Imports (esm.sh — pin 0.185.1; the '0.185.0 stale build' claim was FALSE, but 0.185.1 has the containsBox containment fix)\n\n```js\nimport * as THREE from 'https://esm.sh/three@0.185.1';\nimport { SVGLoader } from 'https://esm.sh/three@0.185.1/addons/loaders/SVGLoader.js';\nimport { mergeVertices, toCreasedNormals }\n  from 'https://esm.sh/three@0.185.1/addons/utils/BufferGeometryUtils.js';\n```\nVerified by fetching all three URLs: the addon modules `import \"/three@0.185.1/es2022/three.mjs\"`,\nwhich is exactly what the root URL re-exports. One THREE instance, no importmap.\n`/examples/jsm/*` returns 200 and is equivalent.\n\n---\n\n## 1. SVG string → Shapes (correct evenodd holes)\n\n```js\n/**\n * @param {string} svgText  raw SVG markup (not a URL)\n * @param {'evenodd'|'nonzero'|null} fillRule  force the rule; null = trust the SVG\n * @returns {THREE.Shape[]}\n */\nexport function shapesFromSVG(svgText, { fillRule = null } = {}) {\n  // parse() is an INSTANCE method and needs DOMParser.\n  // Returns { paths: ShapePath[], gradients: Object, xml: Element }  <-- xml is\n  // documentElement (the <svg> Element), NOT the Document.\n  const data = new SVGLoader().parse(svgText);\n  const shapes = [];\n\n  for (const shapePath of data.paths) {\n    const style = shapePath.userData?.style ?? {};\n    if (style.fill === 'none') continue;\n\n    if (fillRule) {\n      shapePath.userData = shapePath.userData ?? {};\n      shapePath.userData.style = { ...style, fillRule };   // <-- the workaround\n    }\n\n    shapes.push(...shapePath.toShapes());   // r185: NO arguments\n  }\n  return shapes;\n}\n```\n\nRe-verified on three@0.185.1 + linkedom, donut =\n`M100,10 A90,90 0 1,1 99.9,10 Z M100,60 A40,40 0 1,1 99.9,60 Z`\n(both subpaths CCW, ShapeUtils.area +25157 / +4969):\n\n| input                                    | result                                |\n|------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|\n| `fill-rule=\"evenodd\"`                    | 1 Shape, **1 hole**                   |\n| no attribute (defaults nonzero)          | 1 Shape, **0 holes** — solid disc      |\n| `fill-rule=\"nonzero\"`                    | 1 Shape, **0 holes**                   |\n| no attribute + forced `evenodd`          | 1 Shape, **1 hole**                   |\n| plate + 2 same-winding cutouts, evenodd  | 1 Shape, **2 holes**                  |\n| same plate forced `nonzero`              | 1 Shape, **0 holes**                   |\n\nSame donut on three@0.184.0: **2 Shapes, 0 holes each** (with or without the removed\n`isCCW` argument) — the classic z-fighting-disc-on-the-face bug.\n\n### REMOVED: `fixWindingForOldThree`\n\nThe original report's ≤r184 winding-repair helper is **broken and must not be shipped**.\nIt relies on `curve.reverse()`:\n\n```js\no.sp.curves.forEach(c => typeof c.reverse === 'function' && c.reverse());\n```\n\nNo curve class in `three/src/extras/` defines a `reverse()` method (verified by grep over\nthe whole r185 `src/extras` tree — zero hits). The predicate is therefore always false, so\nthe helper only reverses the ORDER of `curves`, producing a path whose segment endpoints no\nlonger chain — silently worse than doing nothing. If you must support ≤r184, rebuild each\nsubpath from `getPoints()` with the point array reversed and construct a fresh\n`THREE.Path`/`THREE.Shape` from those points; do not try to reverse Curve objects.\n\n---\n\n## 2 + 4 + 5. Shapes → centred, Y-up, rounded solid\n\n```js\n/** Swap verts 1 and 2 of every triangle — undoes the winding flip from a geometry mirror.\n *  NOTE: writes the raw typed arrays directly. Do not use on interleaved attributes. */\nexport function reverseWinding(geometry) {\n  const g = geometry.index ? geometry.toNonIndexed() : geometry;\n  for (const name in g.attributes) {\n    const a = g.attributes[name], is = a.itemSize, arr = a.array;\n    for (let t = 0; t < a.count; t += 3) {\n      for (let k = 0; k < is; k++) {\n        const i = (t + 1) * is + k, j = (t + 2) * is + k;\n        const tmp = arr[i]; arr[i] = arr[j]; arr[j] = tmp;\n      }\n    }\n    a.needsUpdate = true;\n  }\n  return g;\n}\n\nexport function buildRoundedSolid(shapes, {\n  R             = 0.35,     // half-round radius; bevelThickness === bevelSize === R\n  slab          = 0.0,      // straight wall between the two half-rounds; 0 = pure capsule\n  bevelSegments = 12,\n  curveSegments = 48,\n  inscribe      = true,     // bevelOffset = -R  (see the CONSTRAINT note below!)\n  targetSize    = 2,        // normalise max XY extent (keeps toCreasedNormals honest)\n  smoothing     = 'creased',// 'creased' | 'merged'\n  creaseAngle   = Math.PI / 3,\n} = {}) {\n\n  let geo = new THREE.ExtrudeGeometry(shapes, {\n    curveSegments,\n    steps:          1,\n    depth:          Math.max(slab, 1e-4),   // NOT 0 — see degenerate-triangle note\n    bevelEnabled:   true,\n    bevelThickness: R,                      // Z half-extent of each round\n    bevelSize:      R,                      // outward XY reach (=== R ⇒ exact quarter circle)\n    bevelOffset:    inscribe ? -R : 0,\n    bevelSegments,\n  });\n\n  // --- SVG is Y-down. Mirror Y, then repair winding (applyMatrix4 fixes normals, not winding) ---\n  geo.applyMatrix4(new THREE.Matrix4().makeScale(1, -1, 1));\n  geo = reverseWinding(geo);\n\n  geo.center();                              // bbox-based\n\n  // --- normalise scale BEFORE toCreasedNormals (hardcoded 0.01-unit weld grid) ---\n  geo.computeBoundingBox();\n  const size = geo.boundingBox.getSize(new THREE.Vector3());\n  geo.scale(...Array(3).fill(targetSize / Math.max(size.x, size.y)));\n\n  if (smoothing === 'creased') {\n    geo = toCreasedNormals(geo, creaseAngle);   // stays NON-indexed\n  } else {\n    geo.deleteAttribute('normal');              // MANDATORY\n    geo.deleteAttribute('uv');                  // recommended (UV-seam note)\n    geo = mergeVertices(geo, 1e-4);             // returns a NEW indexed geometry\n    geo.computeVertexNormals();\n  }\n  return geo;\n}\n```\n\nVerified end-to-end on a real SVG plate-with-square-hole: 0 of 416 triangles have winding\ndisagreeing with their normal; final bbox `(-1,-1,-0.0667) … (1,1,0.0667)`.\n\nObject-level alternative (three fixes the winding for you):\n\n```js\nconst mesh = new THREE.Mesh(geo, mat);\nmesh.scale.y = -1;   // WebGLRenderer.js:1188 — matrixWorld.determinantAffine() < 0 → frontFaceCW\n```\n\n### Bevel geometry cheat-sheet (all re-measured on r185)\n\n```\nz ∈ [ -bevelThickness , depth + bevelThickness ]     total Z = depth + 2*bevelThickness\nLID rings  (b = 0, z = ±bevelThickness): outline offset by  bevelOffset\nBODY rings (z = 0..depth)              : outline offset by  bevelSize + bevelOffset\nbevel ring b:  t  = b/bevelSegments\n               z  = bevelThickness * cos(t*PI/2)\n               bs = bevelSize      * sin(t*PI/2) + bevelOffset     ← quarter ellipse\n```\n\nMeasured, 10x10 square, depth 4, bevelThickness = bevelSize = 1:\n\n| bevelOffset | lid x-range | body x-range | bbox        |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `0`  (default) | 0 … 10 (original) | **−1 … 11** (bulges out) | (−1,−1,−1)…(11,11,5) |\n| `−1` (inscribe)| **1 … 9** (contracted!) | 0 … 10 (= original) | (0,0,−1)…(10,10,5) |\n\n| goal | settings |\n|---|---|\n| exact quarter-circle rounds | `bevelThickness === bevelSize === R` |\n| full capsule, no flat face  | `depth: 1e-4`, `bevelSegments: 12+` |\n| max silhouette = SVG outline| `bevelOffset: -R` (lids then shrink by R) |\n| bevel grows outside outline | `bevelOffset: 0` (default) |\n\n### ⚠ CORRECTED SAFE-R CONSTRAINT (the original report had this backwards for `inscribe`)\n\nThe constraint flips with `bevelOffset`:\n\n```\nbevelOffset = 0        →  the BODY ring expands by R; it self-intersects where a VOID is narrow.\n                          R < min( inradius of smallest hole ,\n                                   half the narrowest concave gap / slot )\n\nbevelOffset = -R       →  the BODY ring is exact; the LID ring contracts by R while holes\n  (inscribe: true)        expand by R, so it self-intersects where SOLID is thin.\n                          R < min( half the thinnest stroke / limb width ,\n                                   half the thinnest wall between a hole and the outline )\n```\n\nMeasured proof (plate: outer 10..190, square hole 60..140 ⇒ wall 50, hole half-width 40):\n\n```\nR = 22, bevelOffset = -22  → lid x-range  32 … 168   ✅ (outer contracted by 22)\nR = 30, bevelOffset = -30  → lid x-range  30 … 170   ❌ the EXPANDED HOLE has swallowed the\n                                                        contracted outline — fully inverted lid,\n                                                        and ZERO degenerate triangles, so nothing errors\nR = 45, bevelOffset =  0   → body x-range −35 … 235  (huge bulge; hole inverted)\n```\n\n### ⚠ REMOVED: `maxSafeBevel`\n\nThe original report's `maxSafeBevel` is **wrong and must not be shipped**. Verified failures:\n\n1. It approximates a hole's inradius as the min distance from the hole's **centroid to its\n   VERTICES**. On the square hole above that gives 56.57 instead of the true inradius 40.\n   The function returned **50.91** for a shape whose true limit is 40 (`bevelOffset:0`) or\n   25 (`bevelOffset:-R`) — an overestimate that silently produces inverted geometry.\n2. It never compares hole contours against the outer contour, so it cannot see wall thickness\n   at all — the constraint that actually binds in the `inscribe` case.\n3. It ignores `bevelOffset` entirely, and the two cases have opposite constraints.\n4. It returns `Infinity` for any shape with no holes and no detected concavity.\n\nI attempted four replacement heuristics (raw point-to-segment, arc-length-gated,\ndensified, and an empirical lid-winding bisection). None were robust: earcut happily\ntriangulates a self-intersecting contour with consistent winding, so the failure is\ninvisible to a winding test, and every chord/arc heuristic is defeated by right-angle\ncorners. **Do not ship a heuristic here.** Instead:\n\n- Derive `R` from the artwork's known minimum stroke width (available from the design\n  source), and cap it: `R = Math.min(desiredR, 0.45 * minStrokeWidth)`.\n- Or offset the contour by ±R with a real polygon-offsetting library (Clipper /\n  polygon-clipping) and assert the result has the same number of rings and the same\n  signed-area signs as the input. That is the only reliable automatic test.\n- Or bisect visually once per asset and hard-code the value.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Which smoother — measured evidence (all numbers reproduced on r185)\n\n```js\n// square 10x10, depth 2, bevelThickness = bevelSize = 0.5, bevelSegments 6\n// at the +X+Y corner of the body layer, position (10.5, 10.5, 0):\n\n// mergeVertices(normal+uv deleted) + computeVertexNormals\n//   → ONE normal: (0.877, 0.480, -0.011)                       ← corner melts\n// toCreasedNormals(geo, Math.PI/3)\n//   → TWO normals: (0.999, 0.000, -0.044) and (0.000, 0.996, -0.087)   ← corner crisp\n```\n\nVertex counts, bevelled disc (r 5, curveSegments 48, depth 2, R 0.6, bevelSegments 6):\n\n```\nraw                                                     8052 verts, non-indexed\nmergeVertices(raw)                                    → 5216   (normal attribute blocks welding)\nraw.deleteAttribute('normal');       mergeVertices    → 1564   (uv seams at 45°)\nraw.deleteAttribute('normal','uv');  mergeVertices    → 1344   ✅\ntoCreasedNormals(raw, PI/3)                           → 8052, non-indexed\n```\n\n**Verdict:** `toCreasedNormals(geo, THREE.MathUtils.degToRad(50..60))`. Neither function can\ncrease the lid/bevel junction (tangent-continuous by construction — the outermost front-lid\nring measures `normal.z = 0.9952`, a one-triangle-wide soft terminator). For a truly hard\nface edge use the built-in groups — measured on a bevelSegments-3 square:\n`[{start:0,count:12,materialIndex:0},{start:12,count:168,materialIndex:1}]`, group 0 = lids,\ngroup 1 = sidewalls+bevel.\n\ntoCreasedNormals scale sensitivity (re-measured; min front-lid `normal.z`, 1.0 = perfect):\n\n```\nscale 1     → 0.9952        scale 0.02  → 0.9410\nscale 0.1   → 0.9952        scale 0.005 → 0.8418\n```\n\n---\n\n## 6. Refraction ShaderMaterial (corrected: opaque output, DoubleSide guard)\n\n```js\nconst refractVert = /* glsl */`\nvarying vec3 vNormalV;   // view-space normal\nvarying vec3 vViewPos;   // view-space position (camera is at the origin here)\n\nvoid main() {\n  vNormalV    = normalize( normalMatrix * normal );\n  vec4 mv     = modelViewMatrix * vec4( position, 1.0 );\n  vViewPos    = mv.xyz;\n  gl_Position = projectionMatrix * mv;\n}`;\n\nconst refractFrag = /* glsl */`\n// three does NOT auto-provide projectionMatrix to the fragment stage — pass it yourself.\nuniform mat4  uProjection;\nuniform float uIOR;        // e.g. 1.45\nuniform float uThickness;  // slab thickness t, in view units\nuniform float uStrength;\n\nvarying vec3 vNormalV;\nvarying vec3 vViewPos;\n\nvoid main() {\n  vec3 N = normalize( vNormalV );\n  vec3 I = normalize( vViewPos );          // camera -> surface (camera at origin in view space)\n\n  if ( dot( N, I ) > 0.0 ) N = -N;         // face the incident ray (back faces / DoubleSide)\n\n  vec3  R = refract( I, N, 1.0 / uIOR );   // eta = n_in / n_out, air -> glass\n\n  // refract() returns vec3(0) on TIR. With eta < 1 that cannot happen on a front face,\n  // but DoubleSide back faces can hit it — and reflect() gives R.z > 0, which the clamp\n  // below would sign-flip. Bail to zero offset instead of producing garbage.\n  bool tir = dot( R, R ) < 1e-8;\n\n  float rz         = min( R.z, -1e-3 );            // floor |R.z| at grazing angles\n  vec3  exitView   = vViewPos + R * ( uThickness / -rz );\n\n  // exact projection of entry vs exit (also correct for orthographic, where w = 1)\n  vec4 cA = uProjection * vec4( vViewPos, 1.0 );\n  vec4 cB = uProjection * vec4( exitView, 1.0 );\n  vec2 dUV = ( cB.xy / cB.w - cA.xy / cA.w ) * 0.5 * uStrength;   // NDC span 2 -> UV span 1\n  if ( tir || R.z >= 0.0 ) dUV = vec2( 0.0 );\n\n  // cheaper paraxial equivalent (perspective only); mat4 is m[col][row]:\n  // vec2 dUV = 0.5 * uStrength\n  //          * vec2( uProjection[0][0], uProjection[1][1] )\n  //          * ( R.xy * ( uThickness / -rz ) ) / max( -vViewPos.z, 1e-3 );\n\n  // Pack RG = view normal XY, B = x-displacement. Keep ALPHA = 1.0:\n  // feDisplacementMap's handling of semi-transparent in2 is browser-dependent\n  // (w3c/fxtf-drafts#113, still open). Emit a second pass/target for the y channel,\n  // or accept a single-axis displacement here.\n  // NOTE: dUV*8.0+0.5 clips outside |dUV| > 0.0625 — scale to your actual range.\n  gl_FragColor = vec4( N.xy * 0.5 + 0.5, clamp( dUV.x * 8.0 + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0 ), 1.0 );\n}`;\n\nexport function makeRefractMaterial(camera, o = {}) {\n  return new THREE.ShaderMaterial({\n    vertexShader: refractVert,\n    fragmentShader: refractFrag,\n    uniforms: {\n      // by reference -> stays live across resizes with no manual update\n      uProjection: { value: camera.projectionMatrix },\n      uIOR:        { value: o.ior       ?? 1.45 },\n      uThickness:  { value: o.thickness ?? 0.25 },\n      uStrength:   { value: o.strength  ?? 1.0  },\n    },\n    side: THREE.FrontSide,\n    transparent: false,\n  });\n}\n```\n\nAuto-provided by three for `ShaderMaterial` — do NOT redeclare:\n\n```\nvertex   : modelMatrix, modelViewMatrix, projectionMatrix, viewMatrix,\n           normalMatrix, cameraPosition, isOrthographic\n           attribute vec3 position; attribute vec3 normal; attribute vec2 uv;\nfragment : viewMatrix, cameraPosition, isOrthographic          ← that's all\n```\n\nThe shader compiles: three r185 wraps every non-Raw ShaderMaterial in `#version 300 es`\nplus `#define attribute in` / `#define varying out` (vertex),\n`#define varying in` + `layout(location = 0) out highp vec4 pc_fragColor;` +\n`#define gl_FragColor pc_fragColor` (fragment). GLSL-1-style source is fine; GLSL-3\nreserved words used as identifiers are not.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Offscreen render → SVG feImage href (corrected filter markup + href setting)\n\n```js\nexport function makeOffscreen(size = 512) {\n  const canvas = ('OffscreenCanvas' in globalThis)\n    ? new OffscreenCanvas(size, size)\n    : Object.assign(document.createElement('canvas'), { width: size, height: size });\n\n  const renderer = new THREE.WebGLRenderer({\n    canvas,\n    antialias: true,\n    alpha: true,\n    premultipliedAlpha: false,\n    preserveDrawingBuffer: true, // REQUIRED if the readout is not in the same JS task\n  });\n  renderer.setPixelRatio(1);           // canvas.width stays exactly `size`\n  renderer.setSize(size, size, false); // false = don't touch CSS style (also required\n                                       //         for OffscreenCanvas, which has no .style)\n  return { canvas, renderer };\n}\n\nlet currentURL = null;\n\nexport async function toObjectURL(canvas) {\n  const blob = canvas.convertToBlob\n    ? await canvas.convertToBlob({ type: 'image/png' })     // OffscreenCanvas (worker-safe)\n    : await new Promise(res => canvas.toBlob(res, 'image/png'));\n  if (currentURL) URL.revokeObjectURL(currentURL);\n  currentURL = URL.createObjectURL(blob);\n  return currentURL;\n}\n```\n\nFilter markup — the original report's version was wrong in two ways: the filter region was\nclipped to the bbox, and the feImage subregion was given in bitmap pixels. Both fixed:\n\n```html\n<!-- The element being filtered is W x H user units. -->\n<svg width=\"0\" height=\"0\" style=\"position:absolute\">\n  <filter id=\"glass\"\n          filterUnits=\"objectBoundingBox\"\n          x=\"-10%\" y=\"-10%\" width=\"120%\" height=\"120%\"   <!-- SPEC DEFAULT; do not clamp\n                                                              to 100% or displaced pixels\n                                                              get clipped -->\n          primitiveUnits=\"userSpaceOnUse\"\n          color-interpolation-filters=\"sRGB\">\n    <!-- x/y/width/height here are USER UNITS (primitiveUnits), NOT the 512px bitmap size.\n         Omitting them entirely defaults to 0%,0%,100%,100% of the filter region, which is\n         usually what you want. preserveAspectRatio=\"none\" stretches the bitmap onto it. -->\n    <feImage id=\"normalMap\" href=\"\" preserveAspectRatio=\"none\" result=\"MAP\"/>\n    <!-- scale is in primitiveUnits; max excursion is ±scale/2, and 0.5 (128) = no shift.\n         Use RGB channels only — never A (see fxtf-drafts#113). -->\n    <feDisplacementMap in=\"SourceGraphic\" in2=\"MAP\" scale=\"40\"\n                       xChannelSelector=\"B\" yChannelSelector=\"R\"/>\n  </filter>\n</svg>\n```\n\n```js\nrenderer.render(scene, camera);\nconst href = await toObjectURL(canvas);\nconst img = document.getElementById('normalMap');\nimg.setAttribute('href', href);                                          // modern, what all\n                                                                         // current engines read\nimg.setAttributeNS('http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink', 'xlink:href', href);  // legacy — note the\n                                                                         // QUALIFIED name\n```\n\n| method | sync? | notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `toDataURL('image/png')` | sync, blocks | + base64 (+33% bytes) + a decode in the SVG. Fine for a one-shot bake. |\n| `toBlob` + `createObjectURL` | async callback | PNG encode off-thread; `createObjectURL` is O(1). Best on the main thread. |\n| `OffscreenCanvas.convertToBlob` | Promise | Whole pipeline can live in a Worker. Best per-frame. |\n\n(No timings — the millisecond figures in the source report were invented and are removed.\nThe ordering is spec-guaranteed: toBlob is defined asynchronous, toDataURL synchronous.)",
        "pitfalls": [
          "FALSE CLAIM REMOVED: there is NO three@0.185.0 packaging trap. I installed it: build/three.core.js has REVISION '185', getInteriorPoint x3, holesFirst x0, and the evenodd donut gives 1 Shape / 1 hole. The only 0.185.0→0.185.1 src difference is one line in ShapePath.toShapes (containsPoint(interiorPoint) → containsBox(entry.boundingBox)) plus four unrelated WebGPU/TSL files. Pin 0.185.1 for the containment fix, not for a revision mismatch.",
          "r185 reads the fill rule from `shapePath.userData.style.fillRule` and DEFAULTS TO 'nonzero'. If your SVG relies on evenodd but the attribute was stripped (SVGO, hand-built ShapePath, inlined path data), a donut becomes a SOLID DISC — the inner ring is `exclude`d and disappears entirely rather than becoming a second shape. Force `userData.style.fillRule = 'evenodd'` when in doubt.",
          "`toShapes()` takes NO arguments in r185; `toShapes(true)` silently does nothing. `SVGLoader.createShapes()` is a deprecated static that warns on every call. `SVGLoader.parse` is an INSTANCE method needing DOMParser, and it returns `{paths, gradients, xml}` where `xml` is `documentElement` (an Element, nodeName 'svg') — NOT a Document, despite the jsdoc saying `xml:string`.",
          "In 0.185.1 the container test is `candidate.boundingBox.containsBox(entry.boundingBox)` — strictly stronger than 0.185.0's point test. A subpath whose bounding box pokes outside its visual container (overlapping or self-intersecting artwork) will NOT be recognised as nested and will become a separate top-level Shape.",
          "THE BEVEL CONSTRAINT IS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT YOU EXPECT WHEN YOU SET bevelOffset = -R. Lids are triangulated from the contour offset by bevelOffset, not from the un-offset contour. With bevelOffset = 0 the BODY bulges outward and holes/slots close (limit = hole inradius, half the gap width). With bevelOffset = -R the body is exact but the LIDS contract by R and holes expand by R, so the limit becomes half the thinnest SOLID feature and half the thinnest wall. Measured: a plate with a 50-unit wall inverts its lid at R=30 inscribe while looking fine at R=22.",
          "BEVEL OVERFLOW FAILS SILENTLY AND UNDETECTABLY. At R=30 inscribe on the test plate the lid contour is fully inverted (the expanded hole has swallowed the contracted outline) yet the geometry has ZERO degenerate triangles and earcut produces consistently-wound triangles. There is no cheap runtime assertion for this — you cannot detect it from triangle winding or area. Validate visually, or offset the contour with a real polygon-offset library and check the ring count and signed-area signs are unchanged.",
          "THE REPORT'S `maxSafeBevel` HELPER IS BROKEN — do not use it. It estimates a hole's inradius as centroid-to-VERTEX distance (56.57 instead of 40 on a square hole), never compares hole contours to the outer contour so it cannot see wall thickness, ignores bevelOffset, and returns Infinity for convex hole-free shapes. It returned 50.91 for a shape whose true limit is 40 / 25.",
          "THE REPORT'S `fixWindingForOldThree` HELPER IS BROKEN — no curve class in three/src/extras defines `reverse()` (zero grep hits in r185), so `typeof c.reverse === 'function'` is always false and the function only shuffles the order of `curves`, breaking the path's endpoint chain. Rebuild subpaths from `getPoints()` reversed instead.",
          "ExtrudeGeometry's constructor already calls `computeVertexNormals()`, so a fresh geometry carries a flat `normal` attribute; `mergeVertices` hashes every component of every attribute, so calling it directly welds almost nothing (8052 → 5216 instead of 8052 → 1344). You MUST `deleteAttribute('normal')` first, and usually `'uv'` too.",
          "Keeping `uv` leaves unweldable seams: `WorldUVGenerator.generateSideWallUV` picks the x- or y-axis PER QUAD via `Math.abs(a_y-b_y) < Math.abs(a_x-b_x)`, so quads flip axis around a curve. Delete uv and regenerate after, or supply a custom UVGenerator. (three declares `attribute vec2 uv;` unconditionally in the vertex prefix, so a geometry with no uv still compiles — it just reads the disabled-attrib constant.)",
          "`toCreasedNormals` has a HARDCODED 0.01-unit position weld grid (`hashMultiplier = (1+1e-10)*1e2`) with no tolerance parameter. Measured front-lid flatness on a disc: scale 1 → 0.9952, 0.1 → 0.9952, 0.02 → 0.9410, 0.005 → 0.8418. Normalise to ~1–10 units first. At the recommended targetSize 2 the grid is still 0.5% of the model, so very fine glyph detail can weld across a crease.",
          "`bevelSize` default is `bevelThickness - 0.1`, NOT independent — set `bevelThickness: 1` and forget bevelSize and you silently get 0.9.",
          "`extrudePath` silently FORCES `bevelEnabled = false` and zeroes all bevel params. No bevel on a spline extrusion.",
          "`getBevelVec` clamps the corner offset vector to length sqrt(2) ('prevent crazy spikes'), so acute corners truncate rather than extend, independently of any self-intersection. A 90° corner sits exactly on the clamp threshold (lensq === 2).",
          "`depth: 0` for a pure capsule produces degenerate triangles where the two body layers coincide — measured 192 (not 768) on a 48-segment disc with bevelSegments 6; the count scales with curveSegments. Use `depth: 1e-4`.",
          "`geometry.applyMatrix4(makeScale(1,-1,1))` transforms normals correctly but does NOT reverse triangle winding — verified: triangle 0's face cross flips to +Z while its stored normal stays −Z (dot = −1). `mesh.scale.y = -1` IS handled (WebGLRenderer.js:1188 checks `matrixWorld.determinantAffine() < 0`). A geometry-level mirror needs a manual winding reversal.",
          "`reverseWinding` writes raw typed arrays with no itemSize/interleave awareness — it corrupts interleaved buffer attributes. Fine on ExtrudeGeometry output, not fine on arbitrary imported geometry.",
          "ShaderMaterial FRAGMENT shaders do NOT get `projectionMatrix` (only viewMatrix, cameraPosition, isOrthographic). Pass it as your own uniform — `camera.projectionMatrix` by reference so resizes propagate for free.",
          "three's built-in chunks define `vViewPosition = -mvPosition.xyz` (surface→camera). Copy that name/sign into a custom refraction shader and your incident vector is backwards. Use `vViewPos = mvPosition.xyz` and `I = normalize(vViewPos)`.",
          "`refract()` returns vec3(0) on TIR. With eta < 1 (air→glass) that is impossible on a front face, so a `reflect()` fallback is dead code there — and on a DoubleSide back face reflect() returns R.z > 0, which `min(R.z,-1e-3)` then sign-flips into a wrong-direction offset. Zero the offset on TIR / R.z >= 0 instead.",
          "The paraxial formula `offset = (R.xy / R.z) * t` needs a sign flip in three.js view space: the camera looks down −Z so R.z < 0, giving `offset = R.xy * (t / -R.z)`.",
          "Packing an offset as `dUV * 8.0 + 0.5` clips for |dUV| > 0.0625 and there is no warning — clamp explicitly and scale the packing factor to your measured range.",
          "NEVER put displacement data in the map's ALPHA channel while also reading its colour channels. Spec says in2 calculations use non-premultiplied values, but w3c/fxtf-drafts#113 is still OPEN and browsers disagree: Chrome/Firefox treat semi-transparent colours identically to solid ones of the same value and fully-transparent as solid black, while Edge scales displacement linearly by alpha. Render the map fully opaque (gl_FragColor.a = 1.0) and use only R/G/B as channel selectors.",
          "feDisplacementMap max excursion is ±scale/2, not ±scale, and channel value 0.5 (128) means zero shift. Both channel selectors default to 'A'.",
          "A filter primitive's x/y/width/height are in **primitiveUnits**, so `<feImage width=\"512\">` under `primitiveUnits=\"userSpaceOnUse\"` means 512 USER UNITS, unrelated to your 512px canvas. Omit them (defaults are 0%,0%,100%,100% of the filter region) or set them to the region in user space, and keep `preserveAspectRatio=\"none\"`.",
          "`filterUnits=\"objectBoundingBox\" x=\"0\" y=\"0\" width=\"100%\" height=\"100%\"` clips the filter region to exactly the bbox, cutting off every outward displacement. The spec defaults are −10%, −10%, 120%, 120% — keep them, or widen further to cover ±scale/2.",
          "`setAttributeNS(xlinkNS, 'href', v)` is WRONG — setAttributeNS takes a QUALIFIED name, so that creates a prefix-less attribute in the xlink namespace that nothing reads. Use `setAttribute('href', v)` (what all current engines honour) plus, if you need legacy support, `setAttributeNS('http://www.w3.org/1999/xlink', 'xlink:href', v)`.",
          "Firefox's feImage does NOT support document-fragment references — MDN BCD carries an explicit note on both href and xlink:href: 'Document fragments (including references to fragments in the current document) are not supported'. `crossorigin` on feImage is Firefox-114+ only; Chrome and Safari do not support it. Raster data:/blob: hrefs are the portable path.",
          "`blob:` hrefs work in an inline-document filter but are not reliable inside an SVG that is itself loaded as an image (SVG-as-image forbids external resource loads and blob: is a fetch). Prefer `data:` if the SVG might be used as an image. This is a downgrade — the original 'both are treated as local' claim could not be substantiated.",
          "Reading the WebGL canvas across ANY task boundary (await, rAF, setTimeout, worker postMessage) without `preserveDrawingBuffer: true` returns a blank image. It can only be set in the WebGLRenderer constructor.",
          "`renderer.setPixelRatio(devicePixelRatio)` silently makes canvas.width larger than the size you asked for. Use `setPixelRatio(1)` for bake targets, and `setSize(w, h, false)` — the `false` is mandatory for OffscreenCanvas, which has no `.style`.",
          "Every `URL.createObjectURL` needs a matching `revokeObjectURL`; in a per-frame loop you leak a decoded bitmap per frame."
        ],

        "code": "```glsl\n// ============================================================================\n// glass.vert  —  three.js r185 ShaderMaterial, glslVersion: THREE.GLSL3\n// three injects (verified in WebGLProgram.js, non-Raw path): #version 300 es,\n// precision, projectionMatrix, modelViewMatrix, modelMatrix, viewMatrix,\n// normalMatrix, cameraPosition, and `attribute vec3 position; attribute vec3\n// normal; attribute vec2 uv;` (with #define attribute in). Do NOT redeclare.\n// Those three attributes are declared unconditionally, so `uv` compiles even\n// if the geometry has no uv attribute.\n// ============================================================================\nout vec3 vVPos;   // view-space position\nout vec3 vVNrm;   // view-space normal\nout vec3 vWPos;   // world-space position (handed to the DOM/refraction pass)\nout vec2 vUv;\n\nvoid main() {\n  vec4 wp = modelMatrix * vec4(position, 1.0);\n  vec4 vp = viewMatrix  * wp;\n\n  vWPos = wp.xyz;\n  vVPos = vp.xyz;\n  vVNrm = normalize(normalMatrix * normal);   // normalMatrix = inverse-transpose\n                                              // of modelViewMatrix -> VIEW space\n  vUv   = uv;\n  gl_Position = projectionMatrix * vp;\n}\n```\n\n```glsl\n// ============================================================================\n// glass.frag  —  GLSL ES 3.00.  No environment map: the \"environment\" is\n// synthesised analytically from the reflection vector.\n// Output is ADDITIVE LIGHT ONLY (the DOM pass owns the body of the glass).\n//\n// FIXES vs. the first draft:\n//  - removed the refract()-based dispersion lobe (it was weighted by Fresnel,\n//    which is backwards, and mix()ing it over the reflection at dispersion=1\n//    replaced the reflection entirely instead of adding a fringe). The colour\n//    split is now a per-channel deviation of the REFLECTION vector, which\n//    collapses exactly to the plain reflection at dispersion = 0.\n//  - removed the `dot(N, H * 0.0 + L)` obfuscation (it was just dot(N,L)).\n//  - moved the tangent T above the env evaluation (it is needed there now).\n//  - no colour-space encode here: this pass renders into a linear RT.\n//  - MRT made optional behind a #define so the material can be drawn straight\n//    to the canvas without writing to a non-existent draw buffer.\n// ============================================================================\n#ifndef PI\n#define PI 3.141592653589793\n#endif\n\n// #define USE_MRT   // define from JS (material.defines) when rendering into a\n                     // WebGLRenderTarget created with { count: 2 }.\n\nin vec3 vVPos;\nin vec3 vVNrm;\nin vec3 vWPos;\nin vec2 vUv;\n\nlayout(location = 0) out vec4 fragColor;\n#ifdef USE_MRT\nlayout(location = 1) out vec4 gGlow;\n#endif\n\n// ---- optics ---------------------------------------------------------------\nuniform float uIor;              // 1.0 .. 2.5   (1.5 = glass)\nuniform float uDispersion;       // 0 .. 1       per-channel spread\nuniform float uDispBoost;        // 1 .. 12      exaggerates the physical 0.025\nuniform float uAberrationPx;     // 0 .. 8       rim colour split, in pixels\n// ---- fresnel --------------------------------------------------------------\nuniform float uFresnelPower;     // 1 .. 8       5.0 = physical, 3.0 = glowing rim\nuniform float uFresnelIntensity; // 0 .. 3\n// ---- specular -------------------------------------------------------------\nuniform float uSpecIntensity;    // 0 .. 4\nuniform float uSpecRoughness;    // 0.02 .. 1\nuniform float uSpecAniso;        // 0 .. 1       0 = round GGX dot, 1 = streak\nuniform float uLightAngle;       // 0 .. 360 deg screen clock angle\nuniform float uLightElev;        // 0 .. 90 deg  0 = grazing\n// ---- rim / bevel ----------------------------------------------------------\nuniform float uRimWidth;         // 0.5 .. 12    PIXELS\nuniform float uRimSoftness;      // 0.5 .. 6     pixels of falloff\nuniform float uRimIntensity;     // 0 .. 4\nuniform float uBevelBoost;       // 0 .. 2\nuniform vec2  uBevelRange;       // curvature lo/hi, e.g. vec2(0.004, 0.05)\n// ---- synthetic environment ------------------------------------------------\nuniform vec3  uEnvSky;\nuniform vec3  uEnvGround;\nuniform float uEnvIntensity;     // 0 .. 3\n// ---- colour ---------------------------------------------------------------\nuniform vec3  uTint;\nuniform float uTintAmount;       // 0 .. 1\nuniform float uSaturation;       // 0 .. 2\nuniform float uGlowIntensity;    // 0 .. 3  (scales the MRT bloom source only)\nuniform float uAlphaGain;        // 1 .. 3  (luma -> canvas alpha)\nuniform float uOpacity;          // 0 .. 1\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- helpers --\n// NOTE: must NOT be named `luminance` — three injects getLuminanceFunction()\n// which already defines `float luminance(const in vec3 rgb)` in this namespace.\nfloat luma(vec3 c) { return dot(c, vec3(0.2125, 0.7154, 0.0721)); }\n\nvec3 sat(vec3 rgb, float k) { return mix(vec3(luma(rgb)), rgb, k); }\n\n// F0 from IOR: ior 1.5 -> 0.04 exactly.\nfloat f0FromIor(float ior) { float f = (ior - 1.0) / (ior + 1.0); return f * f; }\n\n// Physical Schlick (exponent 5).\nfloat fresnelSchlick(float cosTheta, float F0) {\n  return F0 + (1.0 - F0) * pow(clamp(1.0 - cosTheta, 0.0, 1.0), 5.0);\n}\n// Art-directed Schlick: same shape, free exponent. p=5 physical, p=3 glow.\nfloat fresnelArt(float NoV, float F0, float p) {\n  return F0 + (1.0 - F0) * pow(clamp(1.0 - NoV, 0.0, 1.0), p);\n}\n\n// Filament GGX. `a` is LINEAR roughness (= perceptualRoughness^2).\nfloat D_GGX(float NoH, float a) {\n  float a2 = a * a;\n  float f  = (NoH * a2 - NoH) * NoH + 1.0;\n  return a2 / (PI * f * f);\n}\nfloat V_SmithGGXCorrelated(float NoV, float NoL, float a) {\n  float a2   = a * a;\n  float GGXL = NoV * sqrt((-NoL * a2 + NoL) * NoL + a2);\n  float GGXV = NoL * sqrt((-NoV * a2 + NoV) * NoV + a2);\n  return 0.5 / max(GGXV + GGXL, 1e-5);\n}\n\n// The synthetic environment — this is what replaces the missing envmap.\n// R is in VIEW space, so \"sky\" is screen-up and the rig never swims when the\n// object rotates. Vertical gradient + a key softbox on L + a cool fill.\nvec3 fakeEnv(vec3 R, vec3 L) {\n  float h    = R.y * 0.5 + 0.5;\n  vec3  grad = mix(uEnvGround, uEnvSky, smoothstep(0.0, 1.0, h));\n  vec3  Lf   = normalize(vec3(-L.x, -L.y, L.z));       // opposite side, same depth\n  float RoL  = max(dot(R, L),  0.0);\n  float key  = pow(RoL, 28.0) * 1.6;                   // tight bright card\n  float wide = pow(RoL, 4.0)  * 0.35;                  // its soft falloff\n  float fill = pow(max(dot(R, Lf), 0.0), 8.0) * 0.22;\n  return (grad + vec3(key + wide) + uEnvSky * fill) * uEnvIntensity;\n}\n\n// Rim profile: px is distance-to-silhouette in pixels.\nfloat rimAt(float px, float w, float soft) {\n  return 1.0 - smoothstep(w - soft, w + soft, px);\n}\n\nvoid main() {\n  // --- basis. Flip without branching so derivatives stay in uniform flow. ---\n  // `side` is constant per-primitive, so N is still a smooth function across\n  // the triangle and dFdx(N) remains well defined.\n  float side = gl_FrontFacing ? 1.0 : -1.0;\n  vec3  N    = normalize(vVNrm) * side;\n  vec3  V    = normalize(-vVPos);            // camera is the origin in view space\n  float NoV  = clamp(dot(N, V), 1e-4, 1.0);\n\n  // --- ALL derivatives first, at top level (never inside branches) ----------\n  float s     = abs(dot(N, V));\n  vec2  gradS = vec2(dFdx(s), dFdy(s));\n  // s / |grad s| ~= distance to the silhouette in pixels. Huge on flat\n  // camera-facing faces (grad ~ 0), so no rim appears there. Intended.\n  float pxDist = s / max(length(gradS), 1e-6);\n\n  // screen-space curvature = radians of normal turn per pixel. Spikes on a bevel.\n  vec3  dNx   = dFdx(N);\n  vec3  dNy   = dFdy(N);\n  float curv  = sqrt(dot(dNx, dNx) + dot(dNy, dNy));\n  float bevel = smoothstep(uBevelRange.x, uBevelRange.y, curv);\n\n  // --- light rig, view space -> highlight is anchored to the screen ---------\n  float az = radians(uLightAngle);\n  float el = radians(uLightElev);\n  vec3  L  = normalize(vec3(cos(el) * cos(az), cos(el) * sin(az), sin(el)));\n  vec3  H  = normalize(L + V);\n\n  float NoL = clamp(dot(N, L), 0.0, 1.0);\n  float NoH = clamp(dot(N, H), 0.0, 1.0);\n  float VoH = clamp(dot(V, H), 0.0, 1.0);\n\n  // --- bevel tangent: perpendicular to N (so tangent to the surface) and to\n  //     V (so it runs along the silhouette). Length-guarded: cross(N,V) is a\n  //     zero vector where the surface faces the camera head-on and\n  //     normalize(0) is NaN, which would poison the whole composite.\n  vec3  Tv = cross(N, V);\n  float tl = length(Tv);\n  vec3  T  = (tl > 1e-4) ? Tv / tl : vec3(1.0, 0.0, 0.0);\n\n  float F0   = f0FromIor(uIor);\n  float rgh  = clamp(uSpecRoughness, 0.02, 1.0);\n  float a    = max(rgh * rgh, 1e-3);\n  float Fspc = fresnelSchlick(VoH, F0);\n\n  // (1) isotropic GGX — round highlight with the long glassy tail\n  float specIso = D_GGX(NoH, a) * V_SmithGGXCorrelated(NoV, NoL, a) * Fspc * NoL;\n\n  // (2) Kajiya-Kay along the bevel tangent — LINEAR highlight running along\n  //     the bevel band.\n  float ToH    = dot(T, H);\n  float sinTH  = sqrt(max(0.0, 1.0 - ToH * ToH));\n  float expn   = mix(6.0, 900.0, pow(1.0 - rgh, 2.0));\n  float specAn = pow(max(sinTH, 1e-5), expn) * NoL * Fspc * 6.0;\n\n  float spec = mix(specIso, specAn, clamp(uSpecAniso, 0.0, 1.0)) * uSpecIntensity;\n\n  // --- Fresnel reflection of the synthetic env, split per channel -----------\n  // three.js's law: halfSpread = (ior - 1) * 0.025 * dispersion. That constant\n  // is physically right and visually invisible without a textured backdrop, so\n  // uDispBoost multiplies it for the COLOUR SPLIT ONLY. The refraction geometry\n  // (the DOM pass) stays on the mid IOR.\n  float hs = (uIor - 1.0) * 0.025 * clamp(uDispersion, 0.0, 1.0) * uDispBoost;\n\n  vec3 Rf = reflect(-V, N);\n  // Deviate the reflected lobe per channel around the silhouette tangent.\n  // Red deviates least, blue most — same ordering as three's\n  // vec3(ior - hs, ior, ior + hs). At hs = 0 all three collapse onto Rf.\n  vec3 Rr = normalize(Rf - T * hs * 4.0);\n  vec3 Rb = normalize(Rf + T * hs * 4.0);\n\n  vec3 refl = vec3(fakeEnv(Rr, L).r,\n                   fakeEnv(Rf, L).g,\n                   fakeEnv(Rb, L).b);\n\n  float fres = fresnelArt(NoV, F0, uFresnelPower) * uFresnelIntensity;\n  refl *= fres;\n\n  // --- rim: constant pixel width, per-channel offset for the fringe ---------\n  float ab = uAberrationPx;\n  vec3 rim = vec3(\n    rimAt(pxDist + ab, uRimWidth, uRimSoftness),\n    rimAt(pxDist,      uRimWidth, uRimSoftness),\n    rimAt(pxDist - ab, uRimWidth, uRimSoftness)\n  );\n  // Brighten the rim where the surface is actually bevelled and where the\n  // bevel faces the light — this turns a uniform outline into a highlight\n  // that travels around the edge as the light angle changes.\n  float lightGate = mix(1.0, 0.25 + 0.75 * sqrt(max(dot(N, L), 0.0)), 0.85);\n  rim *= (1.0 + uBevelBoost * bevel) * lightGate * uRimIntensity;\n\n  // --- assemble ------------------------------------------------------------\n  vec3 col = refl + vec3(spec) + rim;\n  col = mix(col, col * uTint, uTintAmount);\n  col = sat(col, uSaturation);\n  col = max(col, vec3(0.0)) * uOpacity;\n\n  // RGB *is* the light and is already in premultiplied form; alpha carries\n  // luminance so the halo survives the browser's canvas composite.\n  float alpha = clamp(luma(col) * uAlphaGain, 0.0, 1.0);\n\n  // NO colour-space encode here: this pass draws into a linear render target,\n  // so linearToOutputTexel() would compile to the identity anyway. The encode\n  // belongs in the final composite (see COMPOSITE below).\n  fragColor = vec4(col, alpha);\n#ifdef USE_MRT\n  gGlow     = vec4(col * uGlowIntensity, alpha);\n#endif\n}\n```\n\n```js\n// ============================================================================\n// material setup — three.js r185\n// ============================================================================\n// Build uniforms from the parameter table (the first draft referenced an\n// undefined GLASS_UNIFORMS).\nfunction uniformsFromParams(params) {\n  const u = {};\n  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(params)) {\n    const name = 'u' + k[0].toUpperCase() + k.slice(1);\n    u[name] = { value: typeof v[0] === 'string' ? new THREE.Color(v[0]) : v[0] };\n  }\n  return u;\n}\n\nconst mat = new THREE.ShaderMaterial({\n  glslVersion: THREE.GLSL3,          // required: MRT + you declare your own `out`\n  defines: { USE_MRT: '' },          // drop this if you draw straight to canvas\n  vertexShader:   glassVert,\n  fragmentShader: glassFrag,\n  uniforms: uniformsFromParams(GLASS_PARAMS),\n  transparent: true,\n  depthWrite: false,\n  side: THREE.DoubleSide,            // DoubleSide + a bevel is what feeds dFdx(N)\n\n  // ADDITIVE, not ONE/ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA. The first draft used\n  // OneMinusSrcAlphaFactor as the destination factor, which multiplies the\n  // already-drawn DOM refraction pass by (1 - alpha) — i.e. it darkens exactly\n  // the thing the architecture says must stay untouched. With additive-light\n  // output, black must be a genuine no-op, and only ONE/ONE gives that.\n  blending: THREE.CustomBlending,\n  blendSrc: THREE.OneFactor,\n  blendDst: THREE.OneFactor,\n  blendSrcAlpha: THREE.OneFactor,\n  blendDstAlpha: THREE.OneFactor,\n  blendEquation: THREE.AddEquation,\n});\n\nconst renderer = new THREE.WebGLRenderer({ alpha: true, antialias: false });\nrenderer.setClearColor(0x000000, 0);\nrenderer.outputColorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;\n// antialias:false because we render through an offscreen target anyway (the\n// canvas-level MSAA flag does nothing for render-target passes), and because\n// the SDF-normalised rim is already analytically antialiased.\n```\n\n```js\n// ============================================================================\n// bloom — quarter res, transparent-canvas safe\n// ============================================================================\nconst W = renderer.domElement.width, H = renderer.domElement.height;\n\nconst rtScene = new THREE.WebGLRenderTarget(W, H, {\n  count: 2,                      // .textures[0] = object, .textures[1] = glow\n  type: THREE.HalfFloatType,     // 8-bit bands badly under additive stacking\n  format: THREE.RGBAFormat,\n  samples: 0,                    // NOT 4: MSAA + MRT + RGBA16F is not reliably\n                                 // multisample-renderable across WebGL2 drivers.\n                                 // Supersample the whole target instead if you\n                                 // need edge AA (setSize(W*1.5, H*1.5)).\n});\n\nconst q  = 4;\nconst qw = Math.max(1, Math.ceil(W / q));   // ceil: non-integer RT sizes get\nconst qh = Math.max(1, Math.ceil(H / q));   // silently floored/clamped otherwise\nconst rtA = new THREE.WebGLRenderTarget(qw, qh, { type: THREE.HalfFloatType });\nconst rtB = new THREE.WebGLRenderTarget(qw, qh, { type: THREE.HalfFloatType });\n\n// Shared fullscreen vertex shader (the first draft declared `in vec2 vUv` in\n// the fragment shaders but never supplied a vertex stage to write it).\n// Pair with THREE.FullScreenQuad from examples/jsm/postprocessing/Pass.js,\n// whose geometry is a PlaneGeometry(2, 2) already in clip space.\nconst FS_VERT = /* glsl */`\n  out vec2 vUv;\n  void main(){ vUv = uv; gl_Position = vec4(position.xy, 0.0, 1.0); }`;\n\n// pass 1: bright-pass + downsample (one draw, quarter res)\nconst BRIGHT = /* glsl */`\n  in vec2 vUv; out vec4 fragColor;\n  uniform sampler2D tSrc;\n  uniform float uThreshold;   // 0.55 .. 0.8 LDR, 1.0 .. 1.2 if HDR\n  uniform float uSoftKnee;    // 0.1 .. 0.3\n  float luma(vec3 c){ return dot(c, vec3(0.2125,0.7154,0.0721)); }\n  void main(){\n    vec4 t = texture(tSrc, vUv);\n    // Do NOT un-premultiply here. In this architecture RGB already IS the\n    // emitted light and alpha is a derived luminance, so t.rgb/t.a would\n    // blow up dim pixels. (Un-premultiplying is the right move only for a\n    // conventional coverage-alpha surface.)\n    float k = smoothstep(uThreshold, uThreshold + uSoftKnee, luma(t.rgb));\n    fragColor = vec4(t.rgb * k, t.a * k);\n  }`;\n\n// passes 2 & 3: separable 5-tap linear-sampled Gaussian (== 9-tap discrete)\nconst BLUR = /* glsl */`\n  in vec2 vUv; out vec4 fragColor;\n  uniform sampler2D tSrc;\n  uniform vec2  uTexel;      // 1.0 / targetSize\n  uniform vec2  uDir;        // vec2(1,0) then vec2(0,1)\n  uniform float uRadius;     // 1.0 .. 4.0 texels at quarter res (= 4..16 px)\n  const float O[3] = float[3](0.0, 1.3846153846, 3.2307692308);\n  const float G[3] = float[3](0.2270270270, 0.3162162162, 0.0702702703);\n  void main(){\n    vec4 sum = texture(tSrc, vUv) * G[0];\n    for (int i = 1; i < 3; i++) {\n      vec2 off = uDir * uTexel * O[i] * uRadius;\n      sum += texture(tSrc, vUv + off) * G[i];\n      sum += texture(tSrc, vUv - off) * G[i];\n    }\n    fragColor = sum;\n  }`;\n\n// composite (fullscreen, to the default framebuffer)\nconst COMPOSITE = /* glsl */`\n  in vec2 vUv; out vec4 fragColor;\n  uniform sampler2D tObject;   // rtScene.textures[0]\n  uniform sampler2D tBloom;    // rtA, quarter res, bilinear upsampled\n  uniform float uGlow;         // 0 .. 2\n  void main(){\n    vec4 o = texture(tObject, vUv);\n    vec4 b = texture(tBloom,  vUv) * uGlow;\n    fragColor = vec4(o.rgb + b.rgb, clamp(max(o.a, b.a * 0.9), 0.0, 1.0));\n    // sRGB encode. linearToOutputTexel() IS injected into every non-Raw\n    // ShaderMaterial by WebGLProgram; do NOT use `#include\n    // <colorspace_fragment>` here — that chunk's body is literally\n    // `gl_FragColor = linearToOutputTexel( gl_FragColor );` and gl_FragColor\n    // does not exist under glslVersion GLSL3.\n    fragColor = linearToOutputTexel(fragColor);\n  }`;\n\n// run order per frame:\n//   renderer.setRenderTarget(rtScene); renderer.render(scene, camera);\n//   bright:    rtScene.textures[1] -> rtA\n//   blurH:     rtA -> rtB   (uDir = 1,0)\n//   blurV:     rtB -> rtA   (uDir = 0,1)\n//   [optional 2nd H/V iteration at uRadius*2 for a wider halo]\n//   composite: rtScene.textures[0] + rtA -> screen   (tBloom = rtA.texture)\n// Note the final bloom lives in rtA, not rtB, after an odd number of\n// H/V pairs — the first draft's comment named rtB and would have sampled a\n// stale half-blurred buffer.\n```\n\n```js\n// ============================================================================\n// the slider panel — defaults / min / max\n// ============================================================================\nconst GLASS_PARAMS = {\n  //                        default   min    max    step\n  ior:                    [ 1.50,    1.00,  2.50,  0.01 ],\n  thickness:              [ 0.45,    0.00,  3.00,  0.01 ],  // DOM-pass UV displacement\n  roughness:              [ 0.06,    0.00,  1.00,  0.01 ],  // avoid 0.15-0.65\n  dispersion:             [ 0.35,    0.00,  1.00,  0.01 ],\n  dispBoost:              [ 6.00,    1.00, 12.00,  0.10 ],  // exaggerates 0.025\n  aberrationPx:           [ 1.60,    0.00,  8.00,  0.10 ],  // rim fringe, pixels\n  fresnelPower:           [ 3.00,    1.00,  8.00,  0.05 ],  // 5.0 physical\n  fresnelIntensity:       [ 1.00,    0.00,  3.00,  0.01 ],\n  specIntensity:          [ 1.20,    0.00,  4.00,  0.01 ],\n  specRoughness:          [ 0.14,    0.02,  1.00,  0.01 ],\n  specAniso:              [ 0.55,    0.00,  1.00,  0.01 ],  // 0 dot, 1 streak\n  lightAngle:             [ 135,     0,     360,   1    ],  // deg, screen clock\n  lightElev:              [ 22,      0,     90,    1    ],  // deg, 0 = grazing\n  rimWidth:               [ 2.20,    0.50,  12.00, 0.10 ],  // PIXELS\n  rimSoftness:            [ 1.40,    0.50,  6.00,  0.10 ],  // pixels of falloff\n  rimIntensity:           [ 1.10,    0.00,  4.00,  0.01 ],\n  bevelBoost:             [ 0.80,    0.00,  2.00,  0.01 ],\n  bevelRange:             [ [0.004, 0.05]                ],  // vec2, curvature lo/hi\n  envSky:                 [ '#b8c6e6' ],\n  envGround:              [ '#0f1219' ],\n  envIntensity:           [ 1.00,    0.00,  3.00,  0.01 ],\n  tint:                   [ '#9fd8ff' ],\n  tintAmount:             [ 0.20,    0.00,  1.00,  0.01 ],\n  saturation:             [ 1.15,    0.00,  2.00,  0.01 ],\n  glowIntensity:          [ 0.85,    0.00,  3.00,  0.01 ],\n  alphaGain:              [ 1.60,    1.00,  3.00,  0.01 ],\n  opacity:                [ 1.00,    0.00,  1.00,  0.01 ],\n  // post-only (not shader uniforms):\n  glowRadius:             [ 2.40,    0.50,  6.00,  0.10 ],  // texels at 1/4 res\n  glowThreshold:          [ 0.68,    0.00,  1.50,  0.01 ],\n  blurFrost:              [ 0.00,    0.00,  1.00,  0.01 ],  // DOM backdrop blur\n};\n```\n\n```glsl\n// ============================================================================\n// DOM refraction pass (the OTHER pass) — three.js's own screen-space\n// refraction math, re-verified verbatim against dev. thickness is the one knob.\n// ============================================================================\nvec3 getVolumeTransmissionRay(vec3 n, vec3 v, float thickness, float ior, mat4 modelMatrix) {\n  vec3 refractionVector = refract(-v, normalize(n), 1.0 / ior);\n  vec3 modelScale = vec3(length(modelMatrix[0].xyz),\n                         length(modelMatrix[1].xyz),\n                         length(modelMatrix[2].xyz));\n  return normalize(refractionVector) * thickness * modelScale;\n}\nfloat applyIorToRoughness(float roughness, float ior) {\n  return roughness * clamp(ior * 2.0 - 2.0, 0.0, 1.0);   // inert at ior 1.0\n}\n// exit point -> screen UV:\n//   vec4 ndc = projectionMatrix * viewMatrix * vec4(worldPos + ray, 1.0);\n//   vec2 uv  = (ndc.xy / ndc.w) * 0.5 + 0.5;\n//   float lod = log2(backdropSize.x) * applyIorToRoughness(roughness, ior);\n//   vec4 c = textureLod(tBackdrop, uv, lod);\n// per-channel: repeat with iors = vec3(ior - hs, ior, ior + hs),\n//              hs = (ior - 1.0) * 0.025 * dispersion, taking .r/.g/.b.\n// The backdrop texture MUST have mipmaps (generateMipmaps + LinearMipmapLinear\n// minFilter) or textureLod always returns level 0 and the roughness/frost\n// slider does nothing.\n```",
        "pitfalls": [
          "`gl_FragColor` does not exist under glslVersion: THREE.GLSL3. Verified in WebGLProgram.js: `( parameters.glslVersion === GLSL3 ) ? '' : 'layout(location = 0) out highp vec4 pc_fragColor;'` — the declaration and the `#define gl_FragColor pc_fragColor` are both skipped. Declare `out vec4 fragColor;` yourself. If you DON'T set glslVersion, three still compiles `#version 300 es` and defines gl_FragColor, so GLSL1-style source works unchanged.",
          "NEW — `#include <colorspace_fragment>` DOES NOT COMPILE under GLSL3. The chunk's entire body is `gl_FragColor = linearToOutputTexel( gl_FragColor );` (fetched verbatim). The original advice to add that include as the gamma fix would have been a hard compile error. Correct form: `fragColor = linearToOutputTexel( fragColor );`. The function itself IS injected for plain ShaderMaterial — WebGLProgram's fragment prefix contains `getTexelEncodingFunction( 'linearToOutputTexel', parameters.outputColorSpace )` for every non-RawShaderMaterial — so you never need a hand-rolled `pow(col, vec3(1.0/2.2))`.",
          "NEW — `linearToOutputTexel` is baked per-program from the RENDER TARGET's colour space, not from `renderer.outputColorSpace` globally. A material drawn into a WebGLRenderTarget (default NoColorSpace) gets the identity; only the pass drawing to the default framebuffer gets a real sRGB encode. So call it in every pass and let three decide. Encoding manually in the glass shader AND relying on the composite double-encodes and washes everything out the moment you add post.",
          "NEW — three injects `getLuminanceFunction()`, which defines `float luminance( const in vec3 rgb )` into your fragment namespace. Naming your own helper `luminance` is a redefinition compile error. Name it `luma` (as the corrected code does).",
          "NEW / BLENDING BUG — the first draft's `blendDst: THREE.OneMinusSrcAlphaFactor` directly contradicts its own architecture. With additive-light output, OneMinusSrcAlpha multiplies whatever is already in the buffer (the DOM refraction pass) by (1 − alpha), darkening exactly the thing that must stay untouched, and 'black is a no-op' becomes false as soon as alpha > 0. Use ONE / ONE (AddEquation) for both colour and alpha. It also makes front+back faces of a DoubleSide mesh accumulate light instead of occluding each other.",
          "NEW / PHYSICS BUG — the first draft's dispersion multiplied a `refract()`-derived environment lobe by the Fresnel term and then `mix()`ed it over the reflection by `uDispersion`. Both are wrong: a transmitted lobe should be weighted by (1 − F), not F; and at uDispersion = 1 the mix replaced the reflection entirely, so turning the dispersion slider up changed the whole material rather than adding a fringe. The corrected code deviates the REFLECTION vector per channel around the silhouette tangent, which collapses exactly to the plain reflection at dispersion = 0 and only ever adds colour at the edges. Leave refract() to the DOM pass.",
          "NEW — `layout(location = 1) out vec4 gGlow;` writes to a draw buffer that does not exist when the material is drawn straight to the canvas or to a single-attachment target; the write is undefined and some drivers drop the whole draw. Gate the MRT output behind a `#define` set from `material.defines`, as the corrected shader does.",
          "NEW — `samples: 4` on a `count: 2` HalfFloat render target is a portability trap: multisample-renderability of RGBA16F with multiple colour attachments is not guaranteed across WebGL2 drivers and silently degrades or throws. Use `samples: 0` and supersample the target if you need edge AA. Also `new WebGLRenderTarget(W/4, H/4)` with a non-divisible W produces a fractional size — use Math.ceil.",
          "NEW — the fullscreen post shaders in the first draft declared `in vec2 vUv` with no vertex stage writing it, and its run-order comment named rtB as the bloom source while the ping-pong actually leaves the result in rtA. Both are wired correctly in the corrected code (FS_VERT + THREE.FullScreenQuad, tBloom = rtA.texture).",
          "NEW — un-premultiplying in the bright pass is wrong for THIS architecture. That advice is correct for a coverage-alpha surface, but here RGB already IS the emitted light and alpha is a derived luminance, so `t.rgb / t.a` amplifies dim pixels into false bloom. Threshold on `luma(t.rgb)` directly.",
          "Derivative functions (dFdx, dFdy, fwidth) inside non-uniform control flow are undefined behaviour and produce garbage rims on some drivers (notably Adreno/Mali). Compute pxDist and curv at the top of main() before any `if`, and flip the two-sided normal with `float side = gl_FrontFacing ? 1.0 : -1.0;` multiplication rather than a branch. (`side` is constant per-primitive, so N stays a smooth function across the triangle and its derivative remains valid.)",
          "dFdx(N) bevel detection silently returns zero on flat-shaded or low-poly geometry: N is constant across a triangle, so the derivative is 0 inside every face and only garbage on the edge fragments. The bevel must be actual geometry with averaged (smooth) vertex normals and at least 3–4 pixels of screen width, or the bevel band never lights up and only the silhouette rim survives.",
          "A Fresnel-only rim (pow(1-NoV, p)) has a width that varies with curvature and camera distance — it fattens on flat regions and vanishes on tight bevels, the classic 'plastic wrap' failure. The pixel-normalised form s/|grad s| is what keeps a rim 2px wide everywhere. Keep the Fresnel term for the broad body wash and the normalised term for the line; don't make one do both.",
          "Bloom on a transparent canvas: write luminance into the bloom's ALPHA (UnrealBloomPass does exactly this — `bloomAlpha` from the max RGB channel, verified) or the glow is mathematically present and completely invisible once the browser composites the canvas.",
          "An 8-bit (UnsignedByteType) bloom target bands visibly once you additively stack blurred taps — use HalfFloatType for the scene and blur targets. This is also what lets the rim exceed 1.0 so a threshold above 1.0 becomes meaningful.",
          "three.js's own dispersion constant (halfSpread = (ior-1)*0.025*dispersion → ±0.83% at ior 1.5) is physically correct and visually invisible when your backdrop is a mostly-flat web page. Don't 'fix' it by pushing IOR apart until you see colour — that distorts the refraction geometry too. Multiply the spread for the colour split ONLY (uDispBoost), or express the split as a per-channel pixel offset, and leave the refraction geometry on the mid IOR.",
          "Roughness 0.15–0.65 is a dead zone for transmission — Codrops documents visible pixelation across that band verbatim ('the best results are found in the low (0-0.15) and higher (0.65+) ends of the range'). Clamp the usable range or add a normalMap, which the article says 'greatly reduces the visible pixelation'. Note their 'instances can't be seen through each other' caveat is dated r133 — re-test on r185 rather than assuming.",
          "With mix-blend-mode compositing and a separate DOM refraction pass, the 3D pass must not also paint the body of the glass or you get double-darkening. Output additive light only (reflection + specular + rim + glow) with near-zero interior alpha, and use mix-blend-mode: plus-lighter (or screen) so black is a genuine no-op.",
          "cross(N, V) degenerates to a zero vector where the surface faces the camera head-on, so the Kajiya-Kay tangent must be length-guarded — normalize(0) is NaN and NaN propagates through the whole composite on some drivers. Same for `pow(sinTH, expn)` with expn up to 900: clamp the base off zero.",
          "A world-space light direction makes the highlight swim off the object as it rotates, which instantly reads as CG. With no envmap, put the light rig in VIEW space so the key stays anchored to the screen like a real studio softbox.",
          "UnrealBloomPass's `radius` parameter is not a blur radius: `lerpBloomFactor` = `mix(factor, 1.2 - factor, bloomRadius)`, i.e. it crossfades the mip chain toward the wide mips (verified on dev). If you roll your own bloom, expose an actual texel radius (1–4 at quarter res) instead and don't copy the name.",
          "The DOM-pass backdrop texture must have mipmaps (generateMipmaps: true, minFilter LinearMipmapLinearFilter) or `textureLod` always returns level 0 and the roughness/frost slider silently does nothing — `applyIorToRoughness` will compute a correct LOD that the sampler then ignores.",
          "MeshPhysicalMaterial's documented ior range '1.0 to 2.333' is a doc guideline, NOT an enforced clamp — the source clamps only the derived `reflectivity`, via `clamp(2.5*(ior-1)/(ior+1), 0, 1)`. Values above 2.333 are legal and still bend refraction; they just stop increasing reflectivity. Don't build a slider that hard-stops at 2.333 believing three enforces it.",
          "Verification caveat: all three.js source checks in this report were made against the `dev` branch, not the r185 tag. The behaviours checked (WebGLProgram prefix injection, transmission chunk constants, UnrealBloomPass structure) are long-stable, but pin and re-diff if you hit a mismatch."
        ],
        "confidence": "medium"
      }
    ]
